### §F — v264 NeMo Agent Toolkit (`NVIDIA/NeMo-Agent-Toolkit`) — 2026-08-22 — **NO MINT**

**Counts UNCHANGED: 46 top-level patterns / 12 CONFIRMED Library-vocab. §C-1 = 12. §C-2 = 38.** No promotions, no mints, no retires. `inflation_check` **HELD**.

**Subject:** NVIDIA's Apache-2.0 framework-agnostic agent library (PyPI `nvidia-nat`, CLI `nat`; formerly **AgentIQ** → `aiqtoolkit`). 34 distributions, 321,288 lines of Python, **6,294 test functions**, 493 test files, 131 authors, 84 tags over ~17 months. Wraps **nine** agent frameworks (LangChain, LlamaIndex, CrewAI, Semantic Kernel, Google ADK, Agno, AutoGen, AWS Strands, MS Agent 365) behind one typed primitive (`FunctionInfo`), plus profiling, evaluation, optimization, MCP client+server, A2A, and an 11-skill agent-skill suite with an `AGENTS.md` router. **GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4** [(a) FAIL §41 corporate-not-Anthropic, the v169 SkillSpector precedent · (b) STRONG · (c) STRONG · (d) STRONG].

**§C-2 collision checks, run by hand:**

| Row | Class | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| **C38** | Loop-Engineering Codification Kit (v189) | **ADJACENCY.** The only overlap is budget-cap-and-kill-switch — one bullet of a definition requiring a named methodology, LOOP.md/STATE.md conventions, an L0→L3 ladder and readiness-scoring CLIs. |
| **C50** | Local-First Desktop Agent-Development Workbench (v221 llm-space) | **NOT an instance.** Same *purpose* (instrument + evaluate an agent you are building), wrong **form factor** — a library + CLI, not a desktop workbench, and form factor is the definition's first clause. |
| **C54** | Economic-Survival Agent Benchmark (v233) | **NOT an instance.** `max_budget_usd` is a safety cap, not an objective. |
| **C29** | Agent Protocol Inspector (v173 claude-tap) | ⭐ **The closest call in the registry, and it loses on the LAYER.** claude-tap intercepts the **HTTPS traffic**; NeMo Relay wraps the **process** and emits lifecycle events. Different mechanism, different object. An informative adjacency. |
| **#23** | Pre-Indexed Read-Only Code Knowledge-Graph via MCP (N=4 CONFIRMED) | Not applicable — APP's graph is run-time workflow topology, not a code knowledge graph queried by an agent. |
| **#18 B1-MCP** | agent capability over MCP (≈N=15) | ⭐ **Genuine instance-strengthening** — MCP client *and* server plus FastMCP publishing. Recorded, **not self-incremented** (an N-tally is audit bookkeeping). |
| **#21** | Vendor-Official Agent-Tooling for Own Product/Offering (N=3 CONFIRMED) | ⭐ **Genuine instance-strengthening** — 11 skills + `AGENTS.md` + 26 `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` teaching coding agents to use NVIDIA's own toolkit. Recorded, not self-incremented. |

⚠️ **A FALSE POSITIVE CAUGHT — the fourth consecutive ship with this trap.** The collision grep for `atif` (the toolkit's ATIF trace format) lit up six vault files. **Every hit was the substring inside `RATIFIED`** (plus `stratification`) — the vault says *"the RATIFIED candidate-LLM legibility ADR"* constantly. **There is no prior ATIF/ATOF anywhere in the corpus.** Caught the same way as `ScrollMode`→`llm` (v260), `Plex`→`multiplexer` (v262) and `PageAgentCore`→`agentcore` (v263): **by reading the matched text rather than the match count.**

**A fresh §C-2 mint was considered and DECLINED on four independent grounds.** Candidate class: *"a vendor-published framework-agnostic library that wraps multiple third-party agent frameworks behind one composition primitive and adds profiling, evaluation and optimization."*

1. **Not world-first.** Two independent prior-art lenses returned **"partially"** and **"no"**. LangChain/LangGraph, LlamaIndex, Haystack, AutoGen (Oct 2023), Semantic Kernel, Griptape, Dify/Flowise/Langflow all predate the 2025-03-14 root; the observability/eval layer is Arize Phoenix / Langfuse / LangSmith / W&B Weave / OpenLLMetry territory — **several of which this toolkit integrates rather than replaces.** The narrowest survivable novelty claim (operating at the framework-*composition* level, above LiteLLM's provider level) arrived alongside Google ADK doing something comparable, and is **a position in a stack, not a new primitive.**
2. **The corpus already ruled on this shape.** **v222 lobehub** — world-canonical is not world-first, and fame is not a mint. **v227 hermes-webui** and **v236 dsh-TUI** — a form factor within an existing genre is not a new primitive.
3. **Scale is not a class.** What is unusual here is breadth — 34 packages, nine frameworks, 6,294 tests — not a primitive.
4. **The novel parts are components, not the subject.** The two ideas most worth reusing are the **versioned asymmetric consent record** (`utils/telemetry/consent.py`) and the **per-project isolated-environment test runner** (`ci/scripts/run_tests.py`), and **neither is a capability the toolkit delivers to a user** — both are internal engineering. **v242's D25 applied to a component: the differentiating primitive does not ship as the product.**

Per **§44 clause 5**, **§28 does none of the work here** — grounds 1–4 are each independently sufficient, and no part of the decline rests on anti-inflation.

⭐ **RECORDED FOR THE NEXT AUDIT, NOT SELF-EXECUTED.** **`NVIDIA/NeMo-Relay`** (Apache-2.0, Rust, a separate public repository) is arguably the more novel artifact of the pair — *"visibility into and control over agent runs **without requiring changes to the existing agent stack**"*: a harness-agnostic middleware that wraps a third-party commercial coding agent **from outside the process** and emits normalised lifecycle events, which this toolkit consumes for five agents (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Hermes, OpenClaw). **That is a distinct class from C29's HTTPS-traffic interception.** It is a **separate repository and therefore a separate subject** — a mint decision belongs to a ship or audit that actually reads it, not to this one (**D25**).

**Method note for the routine — ⭐⭐⭐ NEW RULE D51, a new failure mode for this vault's standard assess→refute fleet shape.** Every adversarial reviewer was instructed to *"default to refuted when you cannot independently reproduce a claim."* That instruction **converted broken agent environments into five confident, fabricated refutations**, each carrying an evidence string indistinguishable from a genuine verified negative (no skill has a `references/` dir — **8 do**; three skills have a README — **one does**; `ci/scripts/documentation_checks.sh` *"does not exist, this is a fabricated citation"* — **it exists and holds the ship's best finding**; 24 `.mdc` files — **26**; two skills lack YAML frontmatter — **both have it at line 1**). A recovery agent separately declared `SECURITY.md` and `.nspect-allowlist.toml` nonexistent; both are in the tree. ⇒ **D51: an adversarial verifier told to default to "refuted" cannot distinguish "this claim is false" from "my command returned nothing." A refuter's NEGATIVE needs the same extent-of-search discipline as an assessor's, and the orchestrator must re-run it — a refuter's failure mode is textually identical to its success mode.** ⭐⭐ **And the corollary, which preserves the technique's value: the same run was excellent on the other side of the line**, catching a citation to `type_registry.py:1546` in a 1,448-line file, an inflated *"150+ exports"* against an `__all__` of 94, and a table range wrong by 60 lines. **A refuter is reliable when refuting requires it to FIND something, and unreliable when refuting requires it to FAIL to find something.** **v241's D21** was a verifier that *confirmed* a wrong framing; this is the mirror image. Same root cause: **the verifier's output is a claim, not a check.**

**Streak: `GA:121 · OG:13 [7 ov]`** (44 consecutive goal-aligned, v220→v264); **§35 CLEAR**; no override. Full detail in `_state/03c-projects-v61-v183.md`.

Prose credit is not licence compliance** (D44 inverted).

**⭐⭐⭐ THE METHOD/STRUCTURE CONTRIBUTIONS, for the ~v268 audit.** (i) **A new dangling-pointer mechanism, distinct from v264's:** **97 citations to 26 distinct `docs/design/*.md` documents in a directory that has never existed on any commit on any ref** (whole tree = 2 files under any `docs/` path), including a credential plan cited from 7 files, a multinode audit cited as `P0-2` beside a comment calling the current state *"危险的兜底状态"*, and the ADR named in the CI guard's own failure message. **v264's pointer was ONE comment that went stale in place; these were CORRECT in the repository they were written in and were invalidated BY THE ACT OF PUBLISHING** ⇒ **when you export/mirror/extract a subset of a repo, every internal cross-reference in the copied files silently dangles and no linter in EITHER repo can see it — in the source they all resolve, in the destination nothing knows they were ever supposed to.** That is **v240's inventory rule at the repository boundary**, and it is the sharpest form yet of the v258→v262→v264 transfer thesis. (ii) **The gate ladder completes at a fourth species:** v263 = the test without the gate · v262 = the gate that cannot fail · v264 = the gate that runs, skips the rest and prints which · **v265 = the gate that is wired, would fail loudly, and is never invoked** (4 vitest configs + 5 `"test": "vitest run"` scripts + `passWithNoTests` set nowhere + **zero test files**, against **7 test files on `main`**; no test job in CI — because one would have gone red on day one of v2). (iii) ⭐⭐⭐ **THE SHIP'S RULE, and a genuine advance on v264's:** the asset-ACL default was **moved to `private` in July because nothing broke**; the gateway-auth default **stayed open because the proxy cannot send the header** and turning it on breaks the product — *both* reasoned, *both* written down, same org, same release ⇒ **a discipline travels freely wherever it is free and stops wherever the safe choice would cost something.** So *"do they take security seriously?"* is the wrong audit question; **"which of your safe defaults were free?"** is the right one. (iv) ⭐⭐ **D51 CONFIRMED LIVE AND REFINED:** a refuter disputed my `main` commit count with a `reproduction` string showing `git log origin/main --oneline | wc -l → 50`; the same command returns **108**, as do two other methods ⇒ **a refuter's REPRODUCTION STRING is itself a claim, not a check** — D51 said a refuter cannot distinguish "false" from "my command returned nothing"; **it will also present a wrong number in the format of a verified one.** The same fleet was reliable wherever refuting required it to **find** something (it correctly caught a 954-vs-957 file count, a wrong author count, and two over-claims about files being branch-exclusive). (v) ⚠️ **ORCHESTRATOR ERROR LEDGER — 4, all mine, caught pre-publication, and THREE ARE ONE SHAPE:** I inferred the absence of a **capability** from the absence of a **file** (the README clone URL "broken" — it redirects and works; the installer "making the safe default unsafe" — `0.0.0.0` behind `-p` is normal Docker and it *does* set the variable; Hermes/OpenClaw "unsupported" — they use the generic path by design), and the fourth was v264's `RATIFIED` substring trap recurring (`Zep` in "404 vault files" → **27** on `\bZep\b`) ⇒ **the fix is not "search harder", it is "open the thing the measurement measured"** — the same failure D51 names in refuters, committed by the orchestrator.

PR #5 offers 6 lines to stop it crashing on Windows.** None merged; the maintainer then shipped six more commits to the same two files and, 82 days later, **re-implemented the bilibili feature PR #1 had already contributed** — replacing a public subtitle tool and a written graceful-degradation clause with a commercial third party, a hand-extracted browser JWT and an undocumented endpoint. ⇒ **v265 found that a discipline stops where crossing a boundary would COST something. v266 finds the cheaper failure: the discipline was already at the door, written, reviewed-ready, in a diff on the same remote — and nobody opened it.** Ten minutes of review against three months of duplicated work.

**§F — v267 Obscura (2026-08-23) — ⭐ ONE MINT (§C-2 C57, N=1) + C34 HELD at N=2.** `h4ckf0r0day/obscura` — *"The open-source headless browser for AI agents and web scraping. Lightweight, stealthy, and built in Rust."* A **from-scratch browser engine** that owns its DOM, CSS cascade, layout and paint, embeds V8 via `deno_core` 0.350 for JavaScript, and speaks CDP so Puppeteer/Playwright connect unmodified. 9 crates / **138,078 Rust lines** (`obscura-render` **71,415**, hand-written, no codegen markers) + 33,543 vendored (`taffy` 0.12.1, `cosmic-text` 0.14.2, both forked and declared in `[patch.crates-io]`); **189 CDP methods / 16 domains**; **37-tool first-party MCP server** + `skills/obscura/SKILL.md` + a 202-line `AGENTS.md`; **1,264 test functions**; **910 commits / 1 root / 135 merges / 59 author emails / 13 tags** over **2026-04-13 → 2026-08-21**; 468 deps, **0 git deps**; Apache-2.0. Two clones; HEAD `39fe4d24`. **NOT a fork** — the root commit is the owner's own, so D49 does not apply. **GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4 [(a) FAIL §41 — pseudonymous individual, no declared Anthropic affiliation · (b) STRONG — it *is* agent web-access substrate, first-party MCP + skill, no §40 needed · (c) STRONG · (d) STRONG].** **Counts 46/12 UNCHANGED · §C-1 12 unchanged · §C-2 38 → 39.** `inflation_check` **HELD**; per **§44 cl. 5** §28 does none of the work.

**MINT — C57 "From-Scratch Non-Chromium Headless Browser ENGINE Built as AI-Agent / Scraping Infrastructure," N=1.** Corpus-first for the surface; **decisively NOT world-first** — `lightpanda-io/browser` (AGPL-3.0, Zig, V8, html5ever, 22 CDP domains, native MCP) precedes, announced ~mid-2025 with its `LP` domain published **2026-03-11**, one month before Obscura's root. Capability-not-form-factor (the engine sets the ~30 MB resource envelope that makes one browser per agent task viable), on the v244/v215 reasoning; NO-MINT alternative recorded and declined per the camofox v179 rule that outside-corpus prior art does not create a corpus N=2. Full row at C57.

**HOLD — C34 anti-detect/stealth browser stays at N=2** (v69 CloakBrowser + v179 camofox). C34 requires *"a browser whose PRIMARY reason to exist is fingerprint evasion,"* and Obscura's stealth is an **opt-in cargo feature** (`crates/obscura-cli/Cargo.toml:17` — `default = []`) absent from the default release archive, one row of seven in its own comparison table ⇒ it is exactly the *"general browser-automation tool that treats stealth as one feature"* C34 defines itself against. ⭐ **Third consecutive ship where reading a §C row's own definition prevented a false N** (v262 C37, v263 C38, v267 C34) — the v259 bifurcation's justification now has a demonstrated mechanism three ships running.

**⭐⭐⭐⭐ HEADLINE — the repository implements a competitor's protocol namespace and never names the competitor.** `README.md:403` publishes `| **LP** | getMarkdown (DOM-to-Markdown conversion) |`, expanded nowhere; `crates/obscura-cdp/src/domains/lp.rs:13` implements it; `crates/obscura-js/src/markdown.rs:1` calls it *"the LP.getMarkdown CDP method."* **`LP` is Lightpanda** — per Lightpanda's own blog (2026-03-11): *"`LP.getMarkdown` is the first command in a new custom CDP domain called **LP**, which is home for **Lightpanda-specific** capabilities."* A grep for `lightpanda` across every `.rs`/`.md`/`.toml`/`.yml` returns **zero**. ⚠️ **No code derivation alleged or evidenced** — Zig→Rust, `lp.rs` is ~20 lines calling Obscura's own JS, and implementing a protocol method name is compatibility work with **no licence issue**. The finding is **legibility**, and it is load-bearing because the two projects differ on **AGPL-3.0 vs Apache-2.0** — the single most decision-relevant fact for anyone choosing between them, and the reason Obscura is the first subject in this class that is actually pilotable for hireui.

**⭐⭐⭐ THE SHIP'S RULE — the discipline stops where the artifact stops being code.** v264: a discipline stops at the edge of the team that holds it. v265: it stops where the safe choice would COST something. v266: it was already at the door in a diff nobody opened. **v267: everything a compiler reads here is gated to the best standard in 267 subjects; everything only a human reads is gated by nothing, and `ci.yml:86` says so explicitly.** The code side: a **written threat model** (`ci.yml:6-8`), **12/12 GitHub Actions SHA-pinned**, read-only tokens (`release.yml:8-9`, write confined to one job at `:203-207` that *"never checks out or executes repository code"*), the **trusted-base pattern** (`:54-55` reads `pr_policy.py` and `deny.toml` from the base revision **so a PR cannot weaken the check judging it**), **cache poisoning closed** (`:143-145` `save-if: false`), **rename detection deliberately disabled** (`:92-94`) so runtime code cannot enter through a docs-shaped path, 4 build configs / 3 test configs actually gated (`:147-187`), an **SSRF gate revalidating every redirect hop** (`ops.rs:2234/2331/2572/2820`), and a SHA-pinned external obstacle course run base-vs-candidate interleaved (`:211-217`). The reader side: **`ci.yml:86` routes every `*.md` to zero checks**, and that is where the inflation lives — **four mutually-inconsistent speed figures** (`README.md:35` 85 ms vs `:361-363` 51/84/78 ms vs `AGENTS.md:98` *"~12x"*), **"Anti-detect: Built-in"** (`README.md:34`) for an opt-in feature with **no in-tree detection-suite test**, and the unexpanded `LP`.

**⭐⭐⭐ The number the rule names is computed, displayed, and never enforced.** `AGENTS.md:51-52` and `:69` both state the obstacle course *"must stay **33/33**."* `scripts/ci/compare_obstacle.py` is otherwise rigorous — `:15` `EXPECTED_STAGE_COUNT = 33` enforced both sides (`:65-72`), stage-name set equality (`:73-76`), and it even rejects `bool` for `median_ms` because `isinstance(True, int)` is `True` — then `:91-92` computes `candidate_passed`, `:97` renders it as `| Correct stages | n/33 |`, and `:112-113` makes `if regressions:` the **only** failure condition. **The gate is purely differential: a candidate at 0/33 passes if base was 0/33.** One layer up, `ci.yml:269-279` wraps both runs in `set +e`, captures `base_status`/`pr_status`, and **`printf`s them without ever testing them.** The v256/v264/v265 class, unusually pure because the gate is otherwise excellent.

**⭐⭐⭐ v265's RULE AT INDEPENDENT CROSS-ORGANISATION N=2 — and a stronger instance.** `crates/obscura-mcp/src/http.rs:141-146` **names the attack its own default permits** — *"this stops a malicious local web page from driving the loopback MCP port"* — inside the same comment block explaining why the default stays permissive: *"Unset/empty → permissive (unchanged `*`) so hosted dashboards keep working (issue #175)."* `origin_allowed` returns `true` for `None`; `cors_header` emits `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. **Different author, different language, different domain, two ships after v265 — and stronger, because here the cost and the attack are written in the same comment.** Compounding it: **37 MCP tools with ZERO approval gate, no read-only mode, no URL allowlist** (scoped grep returns only a CORS origin list and the word "confirm" inside a tool *description*), including `browser_evaluate`, `browser_get_cookies`, `browser_storage_state`.

**⭐ A fence made of a sentence.** `AGENTS.md:134-135` tells agents that *"**git-ignored** internal handover notes are private working material: do not … **commit them**. Do not commit generated screenshots or reports."* **`.gitignore` is 18 bytes — `target/` and `.DS_Store`.** Nothing ignores any handover note. And the tree carries the proof: **`build.log`, 283 lines, committed** in `0df158e` (2026-07-24, *"…into pr471-push"*), leaking the maintainer's workspace **seven times** — `Compiling obscura-browser v0.1.0 (/root/obscura2/merge471/…)`, built **as root** in a directory named `obscura2/merge471`. **v258's rule at N=2, inverted:** v258 found an ignore rule *deleted by the commit that added the files it named*; v267 has an ignore rule that **never existed** for files a document says are ignored.

**⭐ What is genuinely excellent (and it outweighs the above in volume):** the **strongest #66 supply-chain posture in 267 subjects** (with pi v228); **1,264 tests that CI actually runs** — not v263 (tests, no gate) and not v265 (gate, never invoked); `AGENTS.md:44-49` documents *why* `nextest` is mandatory **with the mechanism** (*"the engine holds a single V8 isolate per process"*); `deny.toml` distinguishes *unmaintained* from *vulnerable* with a reason and a named transitive parent per RUSTSEC entry, and CI fetches it from the trusted base so a PR cannot add an ignore; **unimplemented CDP methods fail loudly** (`dispatch.rs:557` JSON-RPC `-32601`, with a test at `:812-813`) — the opposite of the compatibility lie I went looking for; **`docs/SUMMARY.md` passes the v240 inventory check perfectly in BOTH directions**, the first subject to do so after v239/v254/v265's 97 dangling pointers; **77 hand-written CSS conformance fixtures** in `render-repros/`; the **cleanest vendoring in the corpus** — MIT / MIT-OR-Apache with all LICENSE files retained in-tree and both forks declared in `Cargo.toml:59-64` with reasons and an upstream exit condition; `Cargo.toml:66-71` pins `panic = "unwind"` with a four-line rationale; and `CONTRIBUTING.md:25-26` sets a calibrated AI policy (*"AI-assisted contributions are fine, but low-quality or unreviewed agent output will be closed"*). **Claude is disclosed in the history** — 6 trailers (`Claude Sonnet 4.6` ×3, `Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context)` ×3) across 4 commits, **all four from outside contributors**; `SGavrl`, author of 660 of 910 commits, discloses none (non-use vs non-disclosure **not established**).

**⚠️ METHOD — a new counting rule, and four self-corrections.** ⭐ **`git log` is a display command, not a counting command:** on this repo under git 2.19, `git log --oneline | wc -l` returns **50** and `git log --reverse | head` reports a false root of 2026-08-08, because the default date-ordered walk terminates early under commit-date skew; `git rev-list --count` returns the true **910** via the pack bitmap. **My first three ground-truth figures were wrong** (50 commits / 7 authors / a 13-day project) **while `rev-list --max-parents=0` had already handed me the correct root in the same tool call.** Also corrected in-flight: a hypothesis that the 59 authors came from absorbed vendored history (**refuted** — `vendor/` has 15 commits; Marc Bachmann's is a genuine Puppeteer `exposeFunction` fix); *"zero AI trailers"* (an artifact of sampling the 400 most recent commits — full history has 47, 6 naming Claude); and *"nothing records the vendored divergence"* (**wrong — `Cargo.toml:59-64` does**, ⭐ **caught by a subagent, not by me**). ⭐⭐ **Three of my five ledger errors were the same shape — concluding from where I chose to look — which is §43.1 for a fourth consecutive ship.** **Fleet: 18 agents, 14 done, 4 died on the StructuredOutput retry cap; 2.26M subagent tokens, 816 tool uses, 1,134s.** ⚠️ **Five of nine dimensions failed** (`cdp-protocol`, `render-engine`, `mcp-and-skill`, `ci-tests-gates`, `docs-vs-code`) — **all five already covered by hand, including the two that produced the headline**; and per **D51** I did **not** adopt the adversary's "zero" figure, re-deriving 6-across-4 by hand. ⭐ The critic's one surviving contribution: flagging *"Anti-detect: Built-in"*, which I had under-weighted. **NOT ESTABLISHED:** whether it builds or passes its own tests (**nothing built or executed**); the 85 ms / ~12× / 30 MB figures (suite in `obscura-benchmark`, not cloned — existence confirmed via CI's SHA pin, contents unexamined); whether stealth defeats any real detection service (no in-tree test); star counts / "10,000 stars" / Trendshift (§37.4 page-stated); the `render` and `ci/fix-pr-check-scope` remote branches. **PILOT: ⭐ READ-AND-BORROW, and the first genuinely pilotable subject in this class — Apache-2.0 where Lightpanda, firecrawl v214, OpenMontage v188 and PilotDeck v175 are AGPL.** Fence: Docker on loopback, **never enable MCP against candidate data or a logged-in session**, set `OBSCURA_MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` if using `--http`, and turn on `--obey-robots` (`context.rs:131` defaults it **false**).

**Two rows tested and both DECLINED on their own written definitions** — the fourth-plus consecutive ship where reading a row's definition prevented a false N (after v262 C37, v263 C38, v267 C34):

- ⚠️ **§C row C42 — "Agent-First Codebase-Documentation Generator/Maintainer"** (N=1, v195 openwiki / LangChain). The subject's `lat.md/` directory is a **597-link, 244-section, 79-code-ref repo-resident agent-readable knowledge graph wired into `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`** — very close on surface. **But C42's definition requires an agent that AUTHORS + continuously maintains the wiki *"refreshed via a git-diff-scoped, human-gated CI loop"*. `lat.md` authors nothing and has no CI loop: it VALIDATES (`lat check`) and RETRIEVES (`lat search`/`locate`/`refs`/`expand`) while the coding agent authors by instruction.** ⇒ **NOT an instance; C42 stays N=1.** ⭐ **Recorded as a DEFERRED WATCH AXIS: the *validate-and-retrieve* pole against C42's *generate* pole** — if a second validate-and-retrieve tool appears, that is a distinct class, not a C42 instance.

**⭐ And the decisive discipline point: `lat.md` is a DEPENDENCY, not the subject.** It is a third-party npm package (**`vercel-labs/lat.md`**, MIT, ~1.8k★, by **`1st1` = Yury Selivanov** — CPython core dev, `asyncio`/PEP 492, ex co-founder/CEO of Gel/EdgeDB, now at Vercel). Minting a class for it would be minting for a tool the subject happens to use — **the v181 cortex-hub discipline** (a thin layer bundling third-party engines earns no mint for the engines). Declined.

**⭐ Reviewable mint alternative, recorded and DECLINED.** *"A community front-end for someone else's open-source agent that verticalises into its own commercial platform — own-brand inference gateway, MCP registry, accounts, cloud sync and a token — while remaining the upstream's client."* Evidence: **seven `hermesone.org` subdomains** (`inference.` ×16, listed **first** in the provider picker; `analytics.` ×12; `api.` ×6; `registry.` ×4 = an MCP registry; `console.` = *"Credits → API keys"*; `www.`/`.hermesone.org`), device-login OAuth, cloud agent sync, a token wallet and a `$HD` token on bankr.bot. **Declined because it is a BUSINESS-MODEL shape, not a capability class** — it belongs near **LV-C2 "OSS-with-hosted-Pro-SaaS-tier-on-MIT-base"** (v78 ECC) rather than in §C. ⭐ **But it carries a genuine twist worth audit attention: v78 was an author monetising THEIR OWN OSS; this is a client author monetising SOMEONE ELSE'S by becoming its default provider.**

**⭐⭐⭐ The ship's method contribution to this registry (recorded here because it bears on how §C rows should be tested, not because it mints anything).** The subject's knowledge graph is **immaculate and ungated**: a hand re-implementation of `lat check`'s four documented rules, swept over **25 commits spanning the graph's entire 7-week life**, found **0 dangling wiki links and 0 dangling code refs at every probe** while the graph grew 7 → 597 edges — **the strongest documentation-integrity result in 268 subjects** — with **no CI job, no npm script, no husky hook, and the tool absent from `package.json`**. The only rule ever broken is the one whose breach breaks nothing (two leading paragraphs at 256 and 258 bytes against a 250 budget, standing 48 and 30 days). And the one enforcement primitive the tool offers, **`require-code-mention: true`, appears 12 times in the repo — all 12 inside documentation about the feature — and is used in ZERO of the 29 graph files**, which is why 2 of 12 declared test specs in `remote-dashboard-oauth.md` have no test. ⇒ **An ungated documentation convention produces documentation that is CORRECT and INCOMPLETE, and incompleteness is the one failure mode no link-checker can see — v240's inventory rule arriving from the opposite direction and confirmed empirically.** The corroborating measurement: the repository's **#2 contributor (187 commits, 159 touching `src/`) has ZERO commits touching the graph.** **Direct consequence for this registry: a future integrity check over `_patterns/06` must be paired with a COVERAGE check in both directions (D15) — verifying that every `C##` row is cited by the ship that filed it and that every ship's mint claim resolves to a row — because a link-checker over this file would report it clean forever regardless of what was never written down.**
